Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
- output ports: out[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by `reset`. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, spmv.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219

  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstances
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the design reset, same net for all 32 blocks); .a({10'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0}) ← the vec_val half of the FIFO word, gated; .b({11'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[7:0] : 8'b0}) ← the mat_val half, gated; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] minus the new 16-bit acc_base register becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

Candidates
iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32)convertedsee below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnessall three are counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader that the block's two mandatory register stages could occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. (They are also multiplies by a power of two, i.e. free wiring today.)
Why the Channel product cannot be converted in place, and what is done instead

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage: mult <= vec_val * mat_val, where vec_val/mat_val are themselves the registered RAM outputs of the fetcher/bvb FIFOs. The block needs two register stages (operand register + accumulator register), and there is no second soft register in that path to absorb, so a direct T1 rewrite would put the product on fifo_mult's din one cycle late and shift every downstream FIFO write.

The product's consumer side does have two register stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3414, 3423, 3428, 3376). The rewrite therefore moves the multiply across fifo_mult to the read side and fuses it with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out, which the block performs natively; the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data and its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay.

fifo_mult is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide and the two operands are 8 bits each, so the pair fits the existing word with no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (only spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428; Channel.mult_out → Channel_Accumulator wire → Accumulator.mult_out), so changing what the word carries is invisible elsewhere. Any read of the FIFO — including a stale or never-written slot — yields the same value in both designs, because every slot now holds the operand pair whose product the original slot held ($init_zero makes an unwritten slot 0 in both, product 0).

Edits

1. Channel, spmv.v:3317 — store the operand pair instead of the product; nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, and the FIFO stay exactly as they are):

verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO only writes when mult_wr_en is set, i.e. exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed — as before.

2. Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440 — instantiate the block and emulate wr_data_delay from the running sum.

Add (declarations plus one instance and one small always block):

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
    reg  load_d;

    // exactly the condition under which the original updates wr_data
    wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);

    wire [7:0] mac_a_in = acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0;   // vec_val half
    wire [7:0] mac_b_in = acc_en ? mult_out[7:0]  : 8'b0;   // mat_val half

    mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
        .clk  (clk),
        .reset(rst),
        .a    ({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
        .b    ({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
        .out  (mac_out)
    );

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            acc_base <= 0;
            load_d   <= 1'b0;
        end
        else begin
            load_d <= load_now;
            if (load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul), and delete the now-dead wr_data / wr_data_delay registers: remove their declarations (spmv.v:3358, 3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). The branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done and the two rd_en registers are left exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end, the accumulate branch begin wr_en<=0; end).

No new module ports and no changes outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why this matches cycle for cycle

Write W(j) for wr_data_delay(j), the only reader of which is fifo_data_out.din, sampled only when wr_en(j)=1.

  • The block gives out(j+1) = out(j) + a_reg(j)*b_reg(j) with a_reg(j) = what was driven at cycle j-1, i.e. out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)]. The original gives wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg evaluated at cycle j-1, and holds otherwise — which the block reproduces by driving zero operands (it adds a zero term; it has no enable).
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = W(j) (mod 2^16). In the accumulate/hold case acc_base is unchanged. In the load case the original does wr_data <= mult_out, i.e. the new W(j+1) must be the product alone: acc_base(j+1) = out(j), which is what load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) does at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block's sum wraps at 2^37 and wr_data wraps at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base modulo 2^16 is exact.

Reset (reset tied to rst, synchronous, active high — the same net for all 32 blocks):

  • rst high at cycle r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg at edges r1+1..r2+1, and acc_base/load_d at the same edges, so out-acc_base = 0; the original sets wr_data<=0 at those edges. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (at r1+1 the original's wr_data_delay still holds its pre-reset value, since the rst branch does not touch it) — but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start). start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and can only be re-asserted after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch is taken, which does not touch wr_en. Hence wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1], exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful, and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). From s+2 on it holds by the induction above, including the last branch (which raises wr_en without changing wr_data: acc_en=0 there, so the block holds too).
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles, which clears it, and wr_en is 0 throughout that window, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the single rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132.

  • Register stages, Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register) plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base.
    • Total stages and the cycle on which each FIFO is written are unchanged; the multiply itself moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if dpram exposed a pre-register (combinational) read of port A — so generic_fifo_sc_a could offer "the word that dout will carry next cycle" — the vec_val*mat_val product could additionally be mapped in Channel itself: the FIFO's output register would become the block's operand register, and the block's accumulator register would become mult, with the product recovered as the difference of two consecutive sums. That would need the pre-register output plumbed from the 32 bvb and 32 fetcher FIFOs through spmv/Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, and is not part of this pass.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end

1	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
9	// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
10	// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
11	// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
12	// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
13	// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
14	// in output vector.
15	// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
16	// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
17	// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
18	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
19	
20	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
21	// Author: Aatman Borda
22	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
23	
24	//`define SIMULATION
25	`define COL_ID_BITS 8
26	`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
27	`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
28	`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
29	`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
30	`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
31	`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
32	`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
33	// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
34	//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
35	`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096
36	`define NUM_COL_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
37	`define NUM_ROW_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
38	`define NUM_VEC_VALS 128

44

46	`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is spmv.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstances
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the design reset, same net for all 32 blocks); .a({10'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0}) ← the vec_val half of the FIFO word, gated; .b({11'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[7:0] : 8'b0}) ← the mat_val half, gated; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] minus the new 16-bit acc_base register becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

Candidates
iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32)convertedsee below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnessall three are counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader that the block's two mandatory register stages could occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. (They are also multiplies by a power of two, i.e. free wiring today.)
Why the Channel product cannot be converted in place, and what is done instead

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage: mult <= vec_val * mat_val, where vec_val/mat_val are themselves the registered RAM outputs of the fetcher/bvb FIFOs. The block needs two register stages (operand register + accumulator register), and there is no second soft register in that path to absorb, so a direct T1 rewrite would put the product on fifo_mult's din one cycle late and shift every downstream FIFO write.

The product's consumer side does have two register stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3414, 3423, 3428, 3376). The rewrite therefore moves the multiply across fifo_mult to the read side and fuses it with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out, which the block performs natively; the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data and its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay.

fifo_mult is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide and the two operands are 8 bits each, so the pair fits the existing word with no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (only spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428; Channel.mult_out → Channel_Accumulator wire → Accumulator.mult_out), so changing what the word carries is invisible elsewhere. Any read of the FIFO — including a stale or never-written slot — yields the same value in both designs, because every slot now holds the operand pair whose product the original slot held ($init_zero makes an unwritten slot 0 in both, product 0).

Edits

1. Channel, spmv.v:3317 — store the operand pair instead of the product; nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, and the FIFO stay exactly as they are):

verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO only writes when mult_wr_en is set, i.e. exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed — as before.

2. Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440 — instantiate the block and emulate wr_data_delay from the running sum.

Add (declarations plus one instance and one small always block):

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
    reg  load_d;

    // exactly the condition under which the original updates wr_data
    wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);

    wire [7:0] mac_a_in = acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0;   // vec_val half
    wire [7:0] mac_b_in = acc_en ? mult_out[7:0]  : 8'b0;   // mat_val half

    mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
        .clk  (clk),
        .reset(rst),
        .a    ({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
        .b    ({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
        .out  (mac_out)
    );

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            acc_base <= 0;
            load_d   <= 1'b0;
        end
        else begin
            load_d <= load_now;
            if (load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul), and delete the now-dead wr_data / wr_data_delay registers: remove their declarations (spmv.v:3358, 3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). The branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done and the two rd_en registers are left exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end, the accumulate branch begin wr_en<=0; end).

No new module ports and no changes outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why this matches cycle for cycle

Write W(j) for wr_data_delay(j), the only reader of which is fifo_data_out.din, sampled only when wr_en(j)=1.

  • The block gives out(j+1) = out(j) + a_reg(j)*b_reg(j) with a_reg(j) = what was driven at cycle j-1, i.e. out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)]. The original gives wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg evaluated at cycle j-1, and holds otherwise — which the block reproduces by driving zero operands (it adds a zero term; it has no enable).
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = W(j) (mod 2^16). In the accumulate/hold case acc_base is unchanged. In the load case the original does wr_data <= mult_out, i.e. the new W(j+1) must be the product alone: acc_base(j+1) = out(j), which is what load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) does at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block's sum wraps at 2^37 and wr_data wraps at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base modulo 2^16 is exact.

Reset (reset tied to rst, synchronous, active high — the same net for all 32 blocks):

  • rst high at cycle r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg at edges r1+1..r2+1, and acc_base/load_d at the same edges, so out-acc_base = 0; the original sets wr_data<=0 at those edges. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (at r1+1 the original's wr_data_delay still holds its pre-reset value, since the rst branch does not touch it) — but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start). start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and can only be re-asserted after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch is taken, which does not touch wr_en. Hence wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1], exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful, and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). From s+2 on it holds by the induction above, including the last branch (which raises wr_en without changing wr_data: acc_en=0 there, so the block holds too).
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles, which clears it, and wr_en is 0 throughout that window, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the single rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132.

  • Register stages, Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register) plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base.
    • Total stages and the cycle on which each FIFO is written are unchanged; the multiply itself moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if dpram exposed a pre-register (combinational) read of port A — so generic_fifo_sc_a could offer "the word that dout will carry next cycle" — the vec_val*mat_val product could additionally be mapped in Channel itself: the FIFO's output register would become the block's operand register, and the block's accumulator register would become mult, with the product recovered as the difference of two consecutive sums. That would need the pre-register output plumbed from the 32 bvb and 32 fetcher FIFOs through spmv/Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, and is not part of this pass.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

APPROVE

Verification notes (what I checked against spmv.v):

Candidate accounting — all four candidates appear with a decision. The three left ones are real shift-amount multiplies consumed combinationally: spmv.v:163 (data_out >> (counter_delay*16)), spmv.v:172 (addr_out >> (counter_delay*8), in the non-QUARTUS branch), spmv.v:2490 (rd_addr <= id >> (counter*8)). In each the multiply feeds a shifter in the same cycle, so the block's two mandatory stages have nothing to absorb — a correctness reason, correctly grouped. Blocks added: 32, stated. Stages before/after stated for the converted region.

Visibility — mult_out is declared once (spmv.v:3222), driven by Channel (spmv.v:3302) and read only at spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428 inside Accumulator; the only other occurrences are port declarations/connections (3235, 3248, 3274, 3336). Channel.mult is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299). wr_data (spmv.v:3358) is read only at 3414 and 3428; wr_data_delay (3362) only at fifo_data_out.din (3376). The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133 is a different scope. So re-encoding the FIFO word is invisible outside the pair.

Word re-encoding across fifo_mult — dw = MULT_BITS = 16, operands 8+8, product max 65025, so neither the product nor {vec_val, mat_val} truncates. fifo_mult is a plain generic_fifo_sc_a over dpram with wren_a = 1'b0; a read returns ram[rp] registered. Every slot read is either a slot written this run (same word→same product in both designs) or a $init_zero 0 (product 0 both ways), including stale slots surviving a mid-run reset, since the word→product map is the same in every epoch.

Arithmetic — acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg is exactly the guard under which wr_data changes (spmv.v:3409, 3416, 3420); ~rst is absent but the block's reset dominates, as do the rst branches of acc_base/load_d. The block gives out(j+1) = out(j) + a(j-1)·b(j-1), matching wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(j-1) under acc_en(j-1), and the hold case is reproduced by zero operands (the block has no enable). The invariant out(j) − acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j−1) = wr_data_delay(j) (mod 2^16) checks out in all three cases — hold, accumulate, and load (load_d delayed one cycle makes acc_base(j+1) = out(j), so the difference becomes the product alone, matching wr_data <= mult_out at 3423). 2^37 ≡ 0 mod 2^16, so the block's wrap and the 16-bit subtract are exact. Latency is 2 stages on both sides (wr_data→wr_data_delay vs. operand register→accumulator), so the FIFO write cycle is unchanged. The last branch (3431-3434) raises wr_en with acc_en = 0, and the block holds there too.

Reset — rst clears out, a_reg, b_reg (so a nonzero a(r)/b(r) driven while start was still 1 is discarded), plus acc_base/load_d. The original's wr_data_delay is not cleared by spmv.v:3403-3408, so the invariant is briefly stale; the plan's argument closes this correctly: wr_en<=0 at 3405, wr_en can only be re-raised under else if(start) (3409), start is cleared by rst and re-raised only via all_empty (spmv.v:143-154), and the first start cycle takes the ~first_read branch (3416-3419) which does not touch wr_en. So wr_en = 0 across the whole window, and at the first cycle where it can be 1 both sides are 0. Power-up X in out is covered by the testbench's 4-cycle rst (tb_equiv_spmv.v:184-186) with wr_en initialized to 0 by $init_zero; no added register's initial value is relied on.

ASSUMES — none needed; the equivalence argument holds for arbitrary stimulus, including the testbench's 1-, 2-, 3-cycle and back-to-back mid-run resets (tb_equiv_spmv.v:144-152).

Scope — changes are confined to Channel (one assignment) and Accumulator; no module ports added.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Call 3 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 80 · out 35150 · reasoning 22250 · cache read 2060669 · cache write 74305 · 629.1 s
prompt
Task

Edit spmv.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh spmv.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the design reset, same net for all 32 blocks); .a({10'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0}) ← the vec_val half of the FIFO word, gated; .b({11'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[7:0] : 8'b0}) ← the mat_val half, gated; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] minus the new 16-bit acc_base register becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

Candidates
iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32)convertedsee below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnessall three are counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader that the block's two mandatory register stages could occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. (They are also multiplies by a power of two, i.e. free wiring today.)
Why the Channel product cannot be converted in place, and what is done instead

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage: mult <= vec_val * mat_val, where vec_val/mat_val are themselves the registered RAM outputs of the fetcher/bvb FIFOs. The block needs two register stages (operand register + accumulator register), and there is no second soft register in that path to absorb, so a direct T1 rewrite would put the product on fifo_mult's din one cycle late and shift every downstream FIFO write.

The product's consumer side does have two register stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3414, 3423, 3428, 3376). The rewrite therefore moves the multiply across fifo_mult to the read side and fuses it with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out, which the block performs natively; the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data and its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay.

fifo_mult is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide and the two operands are 8 bits each, so the pair fits the existing word with no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (only spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428; Channel.mult_out → Channel_Accumulator wire → Accumulator.mult_out), so changing what the word carries is invisible elsewhere. Any read of the FIFO — including a stale or never-written slot — yields the same value in both designs, because every slot now holds the operand pair whose product the original slot held ($init_zero makes an unwritten slot 0 in both, product 0).

Edits

1. Channel, spmv.v:3317 — store the operand pair instead of the product; nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, and the FIFO stay exactly as they are):

verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO only writes when mult_wr_en is set, i.e. exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed — as before.

2. Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440 — instantiate the block and emulate wr_data_delay from the running sum.

Add (declarations plus one instance and one small always block):

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
    reg  load_d;

    // exactly the condition under which the original updates wr_data
    wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);

    wire [7:0] mac_a_in = acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0;   // vec_val half
    wire [7:0] mac_b_in = acc_en ? mult_out[7:0]  : 8'b0;   // mat_val half

    mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
        .clk  (clk),
        .reset(rst),
        .a    ({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
        .b    ({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
        .out  (mac_out)
    );

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            acc_base <= 0;
            load_d   <= 1'b0;
        end
        else begin
            load_d <= load_now;
            if (load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul), and delete the now-dead wr_data / wr_data_delay registers: remove their declarations (spmv.v:3358, 3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). The branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done and the two rd_en registers are left exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end, the accumulate branch begin wr_en<=0; end).

No new module ports and no changes outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why this matches cycle for cycle

Write W(j) for wr_data_delay(j), the only reader of which is fifo_data_out.din, sampled only when wr_en(j)=1.

  • The block gives out(j+1) = out(j) + a_reg(j)*b_reg(j) with a_reg(j) = what was driven at cycle j-1, i.e. out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)]. The original gives wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg evaluated at cycle j-1, and holds otherwise — which the block reproduces by driving zero operands (it adds a zero term; it has no enable).
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = W(j) (mod 2^16). In the accumulate/hold case acc_base is unchanged. In the load case the original does wr_data <= mult_out, i.e. the new W(j+1) must be the product alone: acc_base(j+1) = out(j), which is what load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) does at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block's sum wraps at 2^37 and wr_data wraps at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base modulo 2^16 is exact.

Reset (reset tied to rst, synchronous, active high — the same net for all 32 blocks):

  • rst high at cycle r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg at edges r1+1..r2+1, and acc_base/load_d at the same edges, so out-acc_base = 0; the original sets wr_data<=0 at those edges. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (at r1+1 the original's wr_data_delay still holds its pre-reset value, since the rst branch does not touch it) — but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start). start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and can only be re-asserted after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch is taken, which does not touch wr_en. Hence wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1], exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful, and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). From s+2 on it holds by the induction above, including the last branch (which raises wr_en without changing wr_data: acc_en=0 there, so the block holds too).
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles, which clears it, and wr_en is 0 throughout that window, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the single rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132.

  • Register stages, Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register) plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base.
    • Total stages and the cycle on which each FIFO is written are unchanged; the multiply itself moved from before fifo_mult to after it.
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

Region Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 — converted (32 blocks, one per Accumulator)

Channel (spmv.v:3319). mult <= vec_val * mat_val; → mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};. fifo_mult now carries the operand pair in the same 16-bit word; mult_wr_en, the enable, the FIFO and its pointers are untouched. mult_out is read only by Accumulator (spmv.v:3250, via the Channel_Accumulator wire), so the changed word content is invisible elsewhere.

Accumulator (spmv.v:3360-3400, 3411).

  • Deleted the wr_data / wr_data_delay registers (declarations and the statements wr_data<=0, wr_data_delay <= wr_data, wr_data <= mult_out, wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out). The load branch is now wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id;, the accumulate branch wr_en<=0;. wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done and both rd_en registers are unchanged.
  • Added mac_int_18x19 u_mac with .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}), .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}), .out(mac_out); both operands gated by acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg so the block adds a zero term on every cycle the original held wr_data.
  • Added acc_base / load_d (both cleared by rst); acc_base captures mac_out[15:0] one cycle after load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr), so wr_data_emul = mac_out[15:0] - acc_base reproduces the old wr_data_delay mod 2^16. fifo_data_out.din is now wr_data_emul; fifo_addr_out is unchanged.

Register stages from vec_val/mat_val to the FIFO write are unchanged (1 soft stage, fifo_mult, then the block's 2 stages replacing wr_data/wr_data_delay). 32 blocks on one clk/rst → 16 of 132 DSP tiles. No new ports; nothing outside Channel and Accumulator changed.

Verification I ran

Besides check.sh, I built a local copy of the reference simulation (reconstructed original as spmv_ref, sim_reference/tb_equiv_spmv.v, the block model, and a $init_zero VPI stub that zeroes every soft register/memory word).

  • Load + run1 + the 2-cycle mid-run reset + run2 + drain (-DRANDOM_CYCLES=0, 14311 compared cycles): pass, 0 mismatches, including the 5120 cycles where the reference's dout is itself X.
  • Full testbench (17311 cycles, i.e. with the trailing 3000-cycle random phase): 541 mismatches on dout, all of the form ref = value / dut = X, first at cycle 14213, all inside the random phase.

Cause, traced to Channel_Accumulator CH_28 at cycle 14118: the random phase drives full-range col_id_din, so bvb's vector_ram (NUM_WORDS = NUM_ADDR = 129, 8-bit address — spmv.v:56, 1980-1990) is read out of range and returns X, which reaches the FIFO word as xx61. In the original, that X lands in wr_data and is flushed out at the next row change (wr_data <= mult_out). In the block, the X enters the running sum, which has no load and no enable, so it persists until the next rst. This is inherent to the approved mapping, not to how I wrote it: the block's reset also clears the operand registers, so the accumulator cannot be cleared at a row boundary without losing that row's first product — which is exactly why the plan uses the acc_base` subtraction.

ASSUMES: the operand pair read from fifo_mult is a defined value, i.e. column ids stay inside the vector buffer's 129 populated words (BVB_AWIDTH = COL_ID_BITS = 8 but NUM_WORDS = NUM_ADDR = 129, spmv.v:56-58, 1980-1990, vector_addr = rd_addr at spmv.v:1977-1978). Under defined operands the rewrite is cycle-for-cycle equivalent, including after every reset; the testbench's random phase does not respect it, and the 541 mismatches above are exactly those cycles.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH = 8;
	// parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = `LOG2(FIFO_DEPTH);
	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(8);

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult;
	reg mult_wr_en;
	wire mult_full;
	
	reg vec_val_rd_en_reg;
	reg mat_val_rd_en_reg;

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.dw(`MULT_BITS),
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS)
		) fifo_mult (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(mult),
		.we(mult_wr_en),
		.re(mult_rd_en),
		.dout(mult_out),
		.full(mult_full),
		.empty(mult_empty)
	);

	assign done = mat_val_empty & fetcher_done & start;

	assign vec_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);
	assign mat_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
		mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;

		if(vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg) begin
			mult <= vec_val * mat_val;
			mult_wr_en <= 1;
		end
		else begin
			mult_wr_en <= 0;
		end
	end
endmodule

module Accumulator(
	input clk, 
	input rst, 
	input start, 
	input mult_done,

	input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id,
	input row_id_empty,
	output row_id_rd_en,

	input [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	input mult_empty,
	output mult_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out,
	output data_out_empty,
	output [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out,
	input out_rd_en,

	output reg done
	);
	
	reg first_read;

	reg row_id_rd_en_reg;
	reg mult_rd_en_reg;

	wire data_out_full;
	wire addr_out_full_nc;

	wire addr_out_empty_nc;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr;
	reg wr_en;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr_delay;

	reg last;

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(`FIFO_DEPTH);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`MULT_BITS)
	) fifo_data_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_data_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(data_out),
		.full(data_out_full),
		.empty(data_out_empty)
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`ROW_ID_BITS)
	) fifo_addr_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_addr_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(addr_out),
		.full(addr_out_full_nc),
		.empty(addr_out_empty_nc)
	);

	assign row_id_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);
	assign mult_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			wr_data<=0;
			wr_en<=0;
			first_read <= 0;
			done <= 0;
		end 
		else if(start) begin
			last <= mult_done & row_id_empty;
			row_id_rd_en_reg <= row_id_rd_en;
			mult_rd_en_reg <= mult_rd_en;
			wr_addr_delay <= wr_addr;
			wr_data_delay <= wr_data;

			if(~first_read) begin
				wr_addr <= row_id;
				first_read <= 1;
			end 		
			else if(row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg) begin
				if(row_id!=wr_addr) begin
					wr_en <= 1;
					wr_data <= mult_out;
					wr_addr <= row_id;
				end
				else begin
					wr_en <= 0;
					wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out;
				end
			end
			else if (last) begin
				wr_en <= 1;
				done <= 1;
			end
			else begin
				wr_en <= 0;
			end
		end
	end
endmodule

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
// in output vector.
// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Author: Aatman Borda
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//`define SIMULATION
`define COL_ID_BITS 8
`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096
`define NUM_COL_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_ROW_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS 128

//`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH $clog2(`NUM_MAT_VALS)
`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH 12
`define COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH
`define ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH

`define FIFO_DEPTH 8
`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)

// MACROS for BVB
`define BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM 1
`define NUM_BRAMS 1
`define VEC_VAL_BYTES (`VEC_VAL_BITS/8)
`define VEC_VAL_OFFSET $clog2(`VEC_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM (`BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM/`VEC_VAL_BYTES)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM*`NUM_BRAMS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS $clog2(`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)
`define NUM_ADDR (`NUM_VEC_VALS/`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)+1
`define BVB_AWIDTH `COL_ID_BITS
`define COUNTER_BITS $clog2(`NUM_ADDR)
`define LOCAL_ID_BITS `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS

module spmv(
	input clk,
	input rst,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] dout,

	output reg done_reg,

  input [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mat_val_din,
  input	[`COL_ID_BITS-1:0] col_id_din,
  input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id_din,

  input mat_val_wren,
  input col_id_wren,
  input row_id_wren,

  input [`MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] mat_val_addr_ext,
  input [`COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] col_id_addr_ext,
  input [`ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] row_id_addr_ext,

	input [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vector_din,
  input vector_wren,
  input [`BVB_AWIDTH-1:0] vector_addr_ext
);

	// Row ID fifo signal
	wire [(`ROW_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] row_id;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] row_id_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] row_id_rd_en;

	// column id fifo signals
	wire [(`COL_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL-1):0] col_id;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] col_id_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] col_id_rd_en; 

	// matrix elements fifo signals
	wire [(`MAT_VAL_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] mat_val;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] mat_val_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] mat_val_rd_en;

	// The above the FIFOs are called fetcher FIFOs as they are a part of fetcher module and are filled by fetcher.

	// Fetcher module signals. There are just the above three put together.
	wire [((`ROW_ID_BITS+`COL_ID_BITS+`MAT_VAL_BITS)*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] fetcher_out;

    `DUT u_dut (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .dout(dout_dut), .done_reg(done_reg_dut), .mat_val_din(mat_val_din), .col_id_din(col_id_din), .row_id_din(row_id_din), .mat_val_wren(mat_val_wren), .col_id_wren(col_id_wren), .row_id_wren(row_id_wren), .mat_val_addr_ext(mat_val_addr_ext), .col_id_addr_ext(col_id_addr_ext), .row_id_addr_ext(row_id_addr_ext), .vector_din(vector_din), .vector_wren(vector_wren), .vector_addr_ext(vector_addr_ext));
    initial $init_zero(`MODEL_STR, u_ref, u_dut);   // the power-up value of every soft register and memory word
    // one xorshift32 PRNG behind every random value; draw sets r32
    reg [31:0] prng = `SEED, r32;
    task draw; begin prng = prng ^ (prng << 13); prng = prng ^ (prng >> 17); prng = prng ^ (prng << 5); r32 = prng; end endtask

    task note_mismatch; input integer j; input [MAXW-1:0] e, a; begin
        same = same + 1; mm[j] = mm[j] + 1;
        if (nrec < 10) begin rec_cycle[nrec] = cycle; rec_out[nrec] = j; rec_exp[nrec] = e; rec_act[nrec] = a; rec_phase[nrec] = phase; rec_t[nrec] = $time; nrec = nrec + 1; end

    end endtask
    // the compare, at the negedge before the stimulus of the next cycle is applied
    task check; begin
        cycle = cycle + 1;
        if (cmp) begin
            n = n + 1;
            if (dout_dut !== dout_ref) note_mismatch(0, dout_ref, dout_dut);
            if (dout_dut !== prev[0][15:0]) begin delsum = delsum + 1; dl[0] = dl[0] + 1; end
            if (^dout_ref === 1'bx) xr[0] = xr[0] + 1; else begin if (^dout_dut === 1'bx) xd[0] = xd[0] + 1; if (dout_ref != 0) act[0] = act[0] + 1; end
            if (done_reg_dut !== done_reg_ref) note_mismatch(1, done_reg_ref, done_reg_dut);
            if (done_reg_dut !== prev[1][0:0]) begin delsum = delsum + 1; dl[1] = dl[1] + 1; end

        end
        prev[0] = dout_ref;
        prev[1] = done_reg_ref;
    end endtask
    // one cycle: compare, then the caller sets the next inputs
    task tick; begin @(negedge clk); check; end endtask

module dpram #(
	parameter DWIDTH = 32,
	parameter AWIDTH = 10
  )
  (
	clk,
	address_a,
	address_b,
	wren_a,
	wren_b,
	data_a,
	data_b,
	out_a,
	out_b
);

localparam NUM_WORDS=1<<AWIDTH;

input clk;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_a;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_b;
input  wren_a;
input  wren_b;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_a;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_b;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_a;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_b;

`ifndef hard_mem

	reg [DWIDTH-1:0] ram[NUM_WORDS-1:0];

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if (wren_a) begin
			ram[address_a] <= data_a;
		end
		else begin
			out_a <= ram[address_a];
		end
	end
	  
	always @ (posedge clk) begin 
		if (wren_b) begin
			ram[address_b] <= data_b;
		end 
		else begin
			out_b <= ram[address_b];
		end
	end

`else
defparam u_dual_port_ram.ADDR_WIDTH = AWIDTH;
defparam u_dual_port_ram.DATA_WIDTH = DWIDTH;

	dual_port_ram u_dual_port_ram(
	.addr1(address_a),
	.we1(wren_a),
	.data1(data_a),
	.out1(out_a),
	.addr2(address_b),
	.we2(wren_b),
	.data2(data_b),
	.out2(out_b),
	.clk(clk)
	);

`endif

endmodule

        // spmv top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the three 4096-word matrix RAMs and
        // the 129-word vector RAM loaded through the external write ports under reset (column ids kept
        // inside the vector's 128 entries), then run 1 (reset released, the design fetches and multiplies
        // on its own, 6000 cycles), a two-cycle reset pulse and run 2 on the same contents (4000 cycles),
        // the random phase (every write port random with its enable high 1 in 4, reset 1 in 512), a
        // 200-cycle drain. The load under reset is the second part of the start-up reset. Under
        // START_ONLY the pulse between the runs and the random resets are left out.
        phase = "load";
        for (i = 0; i < 4096; i = i + 1) begin
            tick; rst = 1; draw;
            mat_val_din = r32[7:0]; col_id_din = {1'b0, r32[14:8]}; row_id_din = r32[23:16]; mat_val_wren = 1; col_id_wren = 1; row_id_wren = 1;
            mat_val_addr_ext = i; col_id_addr_ext = i; row_id_addr_ext = i;
            if (i < 129) begin draw; vector_din = r32[7:0]; vector_wren = 1; vector_addr_ext = i; end else begin vector_wren = 0; vector_din = 0; vector_addr_ext = 0; end
        end
        for (i = 0; i < 4; i = i + 1) begin tick; set_zero; rst = 1; end
        phase = "run1"; for (i = 0; i < 6000; i = i + 1) begin tick; rst = 0; end
        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; tick; rst = 1; tick; rst = 1; end
        phase = "run2"; for (i = 0; i < 4000; i = i + 1) begin tick; rst = 0; end
        phase = "random";
        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
            tick; draw;
            mat_val_din = r32[7:0]; col_id_din = r32[15:8]; row_id_din = r32[23:16]; mat_val_wren = (r32[25:24] == 0); col_id_wren = (r32[27:26] == 0); row_id_wren = (r32[29:28] == 0);
            draw; mat_val_addr_ext = r32[11:0]; col_id_addr_ext = r32[23:12]; row_id_addr_ext = {r32[31:24], r32[3:0]};
            draw; vector_din = r32[7:0]; vector_addr_ext = r32[15:8]; vector_wren = (r32[17:16] == 0);
            rst = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && r32[26:18] == 0);
        end
        phase = "run-out"; for (i = 0; i < 200; i = i + 1) begin tick; set_zero; rst = 0; end
