Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is spmv.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstances
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. spmv.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the design reset, same net for all 32 blocks); .a({10'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0}) ← the vec_val half of the FIFO word, gated; .b({11'b0, acc_en ? mult_out[7:0] : 8'b0}) ← the mat_val half, gated; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] minus the new 16-bit acc_base register becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

Candidates
iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32)convertedsee below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnessall three are counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader that the block's two mandatory register stages could occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. (They are also multiplies by a power of two, i.e. free wiring today.)
Why the Channel product cannot be converted in place, and what is done instead

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage: mult <= vec_val * mat_val, where vec_val/mat_val are themselves the registered RAM outputs of the fetcher/bvb FIFOs. The block needs two register stages (operand register + accumulator register), and there is no second soft register in that path to absorb, so a direct T1 rewrite would put the product on fifo_mult's din one cycle late and shift every downstream FIFO write.

The product's consumer side does have two register stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3414, 3423, 3428, 3376). The rewrite therefore moves the multiply across fifo_mult to the read side and fuses it with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out, which the block performs natively; the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data and its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay.

fifo_mult is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide and the two operands are 8 bits each, so the pair fits the existing word with no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (only spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428; Channel.mult_out → Channel_Accumulator wire → Accumulator.mult_out), so changing what the word carries is invisible elsewhere. Any read of the FIFO — including a stale or never-written slot — yields the same value in both designs, because every slot now holds the operand pair whose product the original slot held ($init_zero makes an unwritten slot 0 in both, product 0).

Edits

1. Channel, spmv.v:3317 — store the operand pair instead of the product; nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, and the FIFO stay exactly as they are):

verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO only writes when mult_wr_en is set, i.e. exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed — as before.

2. Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440 — instantiate the block and emulate wr_data_delay from the running sum.

Add (declarations plus one instance and one small always block):

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
    reg  load_d;

    // exactly the condition under which the original updates wr_data
    wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);

    wire [7:0] mac_a_in = acc_en ? mult_out[15:8] : 8'b0;   // vec_val half
    wire [7:0] mac_b_in = acc_en ? mult_out[7:0]  : 8'b0;   // mat_val half

    mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
        .clk  (clk),
        .reset(rst),
        .a    ({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
        .b    ({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
        .out  (mac_out)
    );

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            acc_base <= 0;
            load_d   <= 1'b0;
        end
        else begin
            load_d <= load_now;
            if (load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul), and delete the now-dead wr_data / wr_data_delay registers: remove their declarations (spmv.v:3358, 3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). The branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done and the two rd_en registers are left exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end, the accumulate branch begin wr_en<=0; end).

No new module ports and no changes outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why this matches cycle for cycle

Write W(j) for wr_data_delay(j), the only reader of which is fifo_data_out.din, sampled only when wr_en(j)=1.

  • The block gives out(j+1) = out(j) + a_reg(j)*b_reg(j) with a_reg(j) = what was driven at cycle j-1, i.e. out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)]. The original gives wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg evaluated at cycle j-1, and holds otherwise — which the block reproduces by driving zero operands (it adds a zero term; it has no enable).
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = W(j) (mod 2^16). In the accumulate/hold case acc_base is unchanged. In the load case the original does wr_data <= mult_out, i.e. the new W(j+1) must be the product alone: acc_base(j+1) = out(j), which is what load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) does at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block's sum wraps at 2^37 and wr_data wraps at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base modulo 2^16 is exact.

Reset (reset tied to rst, synchronous, active high — the same net for all 32 blocks):

  • rst high at cycle r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg at edges r1+1..r2+1, and acc_base/load_d at the same edges, so out-acc_base = 0; the original sets wr_data<=0 at those edges. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (at r1+1 the original's wr_data_delay still holds its pre-reset value, since the rst branch does not touch it) — but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start). start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and can only be re-asserted after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch is taken, which does not touch wr_en. Hence wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1], exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful, and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). From s+2 on it holds by the induction above, including the last branch (which raises wr_en without changing wr_data: acc_en=0 there, so the block holds too).
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles, which clears it, and wr_en is 0 throughout that window, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the single rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132.

  • Register stages, Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register) plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base.
    • Total stages and the cycle on which each FIFO is written are unchanged; the multiply itself moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if dpram exposed a pre-register (combinational) read of port A — so generic_fifo_sc_a could offer "the word that dout will carry next cycle" — the vec_val*mat_val product could additionally be mapped in Channel itself: the FIFO's output register would become the block's operand register, and the block's accumulator register would become mult, with the product recovered as the difference of two consecutive sums. That would need the pre-register output plumbed from the 32 bvb and 32 fetcher FIFOs through spmv/Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, and is not part of this pass.

The plan

I'll read the design source and the kept candidate's context before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the single design reset net, shared by all 32 blocks, so two blocks pack per DSP tile → 16 tiles); .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}) ← the vec_val half of the fifo_mult word, ungated (mult_out[15:8]); .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}) ← the mat_val half, gated by the accumulate condition; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] - acc_base becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

This pass keeps the structure of the measured candidate (which stands at A/A0 0.916, D/D0 0.959, product 0.878) and changes one thing: the a operand of each block is no longer gated by the accumulate enable. Everything else — the move of the multiply across fifo_mult, the acc_base/load_d emulation of wr_data_delay, the x bookkeeping, the deletion of wr_data/wr_data_delay — is reproduced exactly as in the kept edit.

iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances)converted (T2)as below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnesseach is counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) at spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) at spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) in bvb at spmv.v:2490). The rewrite (full description; the editor applies it to the unmodified spmv.v)
Why the product is moved across fifo_mult

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage (mult <= vec_val * mat_val, operands already registered FIFO outputs). The block needs two, and there is no second soft register there to absorb, so an in-place rewrite would write fifo_mult a cycle late. The consumer side does have two stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3423, 3428, 3414, 3376). So the operand pair travels through fifo_mult (which is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide, exactly the two 8-bit operands — no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change), and the multiply is fused with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out inside the block: the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data, its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428 via the Channel_Accumulator wiring), so changing what the FIFO word carries is invisible elsewhere; an unwritten slot is 0 in both designs ($init_zero), product 0.

Edit 1 — Channel, spmv.v:3317
verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

Nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg enable, the FIFO are untouched). mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO writes exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed, as before.

Edit 2 — Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440

Delete the declarations of wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423), wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, both rd_en registers and the whole branch structure exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end). Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul). Add:

verilog
	wire [36:0] mac_out;
	reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
	reg  load_d;

	// x bookkeeping, as in the measured candidate: the original's product is all-x when
	// either operand has an x bit; here the FIFO word carries the operand pair, so that
	// case is "this word has an x bit". The block's running sum has no load and no enable,
	// so an x that entered it could never leave, while the original's wr_data drops its x
	// at the next row load; the x is therefore kept out of the block and carried by
	// x_data/x_data_delay. Under synthesis x_mult is constant 0 and all of this folds away.
`ifdef SYNTHESIS
	wire x_mult = 1'b0;
`else
	wire x_mult = ((^mult_out) === 1'bx);
`endif
	reg x_data;        // 1 when the original's wr_data would be all-x
	reg x_data_delay;  // 1 when the original's wr_data_delay would be all-x

	// exactly the condition under which the original updated wr_data
	wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
	wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
	wire mac_en   = acc_en & (~x_mult);

	// CHANGE FROM THE MEASURED CANDIDATE: `a` is not gated by mac_en. Zeroing `b` alone
	// already makes the block's term 0 on a non-accumulate cycle, so the 8 gating LUTs on
	// the `a` side (8 per channel, 256 over the design) are dropped. The x_mult term is
	// the only guard left on `a`, and it is constant 0 in synthesis, so `a` becomes plain
	// wires from the FIFO word to the block.
	wire [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_a_in = x_mult ? {`VEC_VAL_BITS{1'b0}} : mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS];
	wire [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_b_in = mac_en ? mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] : {`MAT_VAL_BITS{1'b0}};

	mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
		.clk(clk),
		.reset(rst),
		.a({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
		.b({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
		.out(mac_out)
	);

	// out - acc_base reproduces the original wr_data_delay (mod 2^MULT_BITS)
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = x_data_delay ? {`MULT_BITS{1'bx}}
	                                                  : (mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			acc_base <= 0;
			load_d   <= 0;
		end
		else begin
			load_d <= load_now;
			if(load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
		end
	end

and in the existing always block put x_data <= 1'b0; where wr_data<=0; was (rst branch), x_data_delay <= x_data; where wr_data_delay <= wr_data; was, x_data <= x_mult; where wr_data <= mult_out; was, and x_data <= x_data | x_mult; where wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; was.

No new module ports, no change outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why the ungated a is still cycle-for-cycle identical

The block's term is a_reg*b_reg. On any cycle with mac_en = 0 the editor drives b = 0, so b_reg = 0 at the next edge and the term added is a_reg*0 = 0 whatever a_reg holds — the same zero term the gated version added. a_reg is never read except through that product. The only case where a stale a_reg could matter in simulation is an x operand, and a is still forced to 0 whenever x_mult is 1, so a_reg is never x and a_reg*0 never evaluates to x. On a cycle with mac_en = 1 both operands are the FIFO word's halves, exactly as before. The block's two register stages, the accumulate timing, and every other signal are unchanged, so the equivalence argument of the measured candidate carries over verbatim:

  • out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)], against the original's wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition; the block has no enable and reproduces "hold" by adding a zero term.
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = wr_data_delay(j) (mod 2^16). acc_base is unchanged on accumulate/hold; on a load the original does wr_data <= mult_out, so the new base must be out(j), which load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) captures at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block wraps at 2^37, wr_data at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base mod 2^16 is exact.
  • Reset (reset ← rst, synchronous active high, same net for all 32 blocks): a high rst at cycles r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg, acc_base and load_d at edges r1+1..r2+1, matching the original's wr_data<=0. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (the original's wr_data_delay keeps its pre-reset value at r1+1, since the rst branch does not touch it), but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start); start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and only re-asserts after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch runs and does not touch wr_en. So wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1] — exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful — and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). The last branch raises wr_en without changing wr_data, and there acc_en=0, so the block holds too.
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles (before comparison starts) and wr_en is 0 throughout, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and register stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the one rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132 — unchanged from the measured candidate, so the expected delta is soft logic only (256 LUT2s of operand gating removed, ≈26 CLBs).

  • Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register), plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base on the FIFO's din.
    • Total stages, and the cycle on which each FIFO is written, are unchanged; the multiply moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

Note on where the remaining delay is: at seed 1 the reported worst path is entirely soft (an enabled flop through five .names levels into a top-level flop, dominated by wire delay — the global |fetcher_empty / &done style reductions in spmv). It does not touch the block, so no block-side change can shorten it; only the density gained from fewer tiles and less soft logic can.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if dpram exposed a combinational (pre-register) read of port A, so generic_fifo_sc_a could offer "the word dout will carry next cycle", the vec_val*mat_val product could be mapped inside Channel itself: the FIFO's output register would become the block's operand register and the block's accumulator register would become mult, with the product recovered as the difference of two consecutive sums. That needs the pre-register output plumbed from the 32 bvb and 32 fetcher FIFOs through spmv/Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, and is a change to the design, not to this rewrite.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

APPROVE

Checked against /work/spmv.v and sim_reference/:

  • Candidate coverage — all four ids appear with a decision; the three counter*CONST ones are left for a correctness reason, and that reason holds: spmv.v:163 and spmv.v:172 are continuous assigns whose product feeds a combinational shifter in the same cycle (module spmv, starts line 61), and spmv.v:2490 (rd_addr <= id >> (counter*COL_ID_BITS), module bvb, starts line 1935) consumes the product combinationally before its one register. COL_ID_BITS=8, MULT_BITS=16, ROW_ID_BITS`=8, so the shift-amount arithmetic cited is accurate.
  • Blocks and stages — 32 blocks stated, 16 of 132 tiles (all on the one rst net via Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, spmv.v:274-277, 3225-3256); stages before (1 soft + FIFO + 2 soft) and after (1 soft + FIFO + 2 block) are stated and match spmv.v:3317, 3414, 3423, 3428, 3376.
  • Visibility — mult is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299); mult_out leaves Channel only to Accumulator (spmv.v:3222, 3235, 3248) and is read only at 3423/3428; wr_data/wr_data_delay are read only at 3376/3414. Carrying {vec_val, mat_val} in the 16-bit word is therefore invisible outside, and 8+8 fits dw=MULT_BITS` exactly.
  • Mapping/behavior — .a({10'b0,·})=18, .b({11'b0,·})=19, out[36:0]; the invariant out(t)−acc_base(t) = wr_data(t−1) = wr_data_delay(t) holds in the load/accumulate/hold cases, and wr_data_delay(t)=wr_data(t−1) is safe because start (spmv.v:143-154) only ever rises between resets. Products ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, so the mod-2^16 subtraction is exact.
  • The one change from the measured candidate (ungated a) — sound: b is still forced to 0 whenever mac_en=0, and the model's prod = a_reg*b_reg is 0 for any defined a_reg; a keeps the x_mult guard, so a_reg is never X and a_reg*0 never evaluates to X. On mac_en=1 both halves come from the same mult_out, so loads/accumulates are unchanged.
  • Reset — .reset(rst) with the design's synchronous active-high rst; acc_en omitting ~rst is harmless since the block, acc_base and load_d all clear at that same edge, and the wr_en=0 window from the reset edge through the first start cycle (spmv.v:3405, 3416-3419) covers the one cycle where the emulated din differs from the un-reset wr_data_delay. X bookkeeping (x_data, x_data_delay) reproduces the original's all-X wr_data exactly and is cleared at each row load; no added register's power-up value is relied on where wr_en can be 1.
  • ASSUMES — none needed; the equivalence argument does not depend on input bounds, and the testbench's mid-run resets (drive_spmv.vh:17, 25) are covered. The plan's "first 4 cycles" description of the start-up reset understates it (rst is held through the 4096-cycle load plus 4 more, drive_spmv.vh:9-15), which only strengthens the power-up argument.

output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit spmv.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh spmv.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source and the kept candidate's context before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the single design reset net, shared by all 32 blocks, so two blocks pack per DSP tile → 16 tiles); .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}) ← the vec_val half of the fifo_mult word, ungated (mult_out[15:8]); .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}) ← the mat_val half, gated by the accumulate condition; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] - acc_base becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

This pass keeps the structure of the measured candidate (which stands at A/A0 0.916, D/D0 0.959, product 0.878) and changes one thing: the a operand of each block is no longer gated by the accumulate enable. Everything else — the move of the multiply across fifo_mult, the acc_base/load_d emulation of wr_data_delay, the x bookkeeping, the deletion of wr_data/wr_data_delay — is reproduced exactly as in the kept edit.

Candidates
iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances)converted (T2)as below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnesseach is counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) at spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) at spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) in bvb at spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader for the block's two mandatory register stages to occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. They are also multiplies by a power of two (free wiring today), so converting them could only add a DSP tile and logic.
The rewrite (full description; the editor applies it to the unmodified spmv.v)
Why the product is moved across fifo_mult

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage (mult <= vec_val * mat_val, operands already registered FIFO outputs). The block needs two, and there is no second soft register there to absorb, so an in-place rewrite would write fifo_mult a cycle late. The consumer side does have two stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3423, 3428, 3414, 3376). So the operand pair travels through fifo_mult (which is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide, exactly the two 8-bit operands — no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change), and the multiply is fused with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out inside the block: the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data, its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428 via the Channel_Accumulator wiring), so changing what the FIFO word carries is invisible elsewhere; an unwritten slot is 0 in both designs ($init_zero), product 0.

Edit 1 — Channel, spmv.v:3317
verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

Nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg enable, the FIFO are untouched). mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO writes exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed, as before.

Edit 2 — Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440

Delete the declarations of wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423), wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, both rd_en registers and the whole branch structure exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end). Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul). Add:

verilog
	wire [36:0] mac_out;
	reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
	reg  load_d;

	// x bookkeeping, as in the measured candidate: the original's product is all-x when
	// either operand has an x bit; here the FIFO word carries the operand pair, so that
	// case is "this word has an x bit". The block's running sum has no load and no enable,
	// so an x that entered it could never leave, while the original's wr_data drops its x
	// at the next row load; the x is therefore kept out of the block and carried by
	// x_data/x_data_delay. Under synthesis x_mult is constant 0 and all of this folds away.
`ifdef SYNTHESIS
	wire x_mult = 1'b0;
`else
	wire x_mult = ((^mult_out) === 1'bx);
`endif
	reg x_data;        // 1 when the original's wr_data would be all-x
	reg x_data_delay;  // 1 when the original's wr_data_delay would be all-x

	// exactly the condition under which the original updated wr_data
	wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
	wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
	wire mac_en   = acc_en & (~x_mult);

	// CHANGE FROM THE MEASURED CANDIDATE: `a` is not gated by mac_en. Zeroing `b` alone
	// already makes the block's term 0 on a non-accumulate cycle, so the 8 gating LUTs on
	// the `a` side (8 per channel, 256 over the design) are dropped. The x_mult term is
	// the only guard left on `a`, and it is constant 0 in synthesis, so `a` becomes plain
	// wires from the FIFO word to the block.
	wire [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_a_in = x_mult ? {`VEC_VAL_BITS{1'b0}} : mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS];
	wire [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_b_in = mac_en ? mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] : {`MAT_VAL_BITS{1'b0}};

	mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
		.clk(clk),
		.reset(rst),
		.a({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
		.b({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
		.out(mac_out)
	);

	// out - acc_base reproduces the original wr_data_delay (mod 2^MULT_BITS)
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = x_data_delay ? {`MULT_BITS{1'bx}}
	                                                  : (mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			acc_base <= 0;
			load_d   <= 0;
		end
		else begin
			load_d <= load_now;
			if(load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
		end
	end

and in the existing always block put x_data <= 1'b0; where wr_data<=0; was (rst branch), x_data_delay <= x_data; where wr_data_delay <= wr_data; was, x_data <= x_mult; where wr_data <= mult_out; was, and x_data <= x_data | x_mult; where wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; was.

No new module ports, no change outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why the ungated a is still cycle-for-cycle identical

The block's term is a_reg*b_reg. On any cycle with mac_en = 0 the editor drives b = 0, so b_reg = 0 at the next edge and the term added is a_reg*0 = 0 whatever a_reg holds — the same zero term the gated version added. a_reg is never read except through that product. The only case where a stale a_reg could matter in simulation is an x operand, and a is still forced to 0 whenever x_mult is 1, so a_reg is never x and a_reg*0 never evaluates to x. On a cycle with mac_en = 1 both operands are the FIFO word's halves, exactly as before. The block's two register stages, the accumulate timing, and every other signal are unchanged, so the equivalence argument of the measured candidate carries over verbatim:

  • out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)], against the original's wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition; the block has no enable and reproduces "hold" by adding a zero term.
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = wr_data_delay(j) (mod 2^16). acc_base is unchanged on accumulate/hold; on a load the original does wr_data <= mult_out, so the new base must be out(j), which load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) captures at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block wraps at 2^37, wr_data at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base mod 2^16 is exact.
  • Reset (reset ← rst, synchronous active high, same net for all 32 blocks): a high rst at cycles r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg, acc_base and load_d at edges r1+1..r2+1, matching the original's wr_data<=0. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (the original's wr_data_delay keeps its pre-reset value at r1+1, since the rst branch does not touch it), but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start); start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and only re-asserts after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch runs and does not touch wr_en. So wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1] — exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful — and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). The last branch raises wr_en without changing wr_data, and there acc_en=0, so the block holds too.
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles (before comparison starts) and wr_en is 0 throughout, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and register stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the one rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132 — unchanged from the measured candidate, so the expected delta is soft logic only (256 LUT2s of operand gating removed, ≈26 CLBs).

  • Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register), plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base on the FIFO's din.
    • Total stages, and the cycle on which each FIFO is written, are unchanged; the multiply moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

Note on where the remaining delay is: at seed 1 the reported worst path is entirely soft (an enabled flop through five .names levels into a top-level flop, dominated by wire delay — the global |fetcher_empty / &done style reductions in spmv). It does not touch the block, so no block-side change can shorten it; only the density gained from fewer tiles and less soft logic can.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

Changes, region by region

Region Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 — the multiply itself (module Channel)

  • spmv.v:3317: mult <= vec_val * mat_val; → mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};. fifo_mult now carries the 8+8 operand pair instead of the 16-bit product; width, pointers, we/re, full/empty and the write cycle are unchanged, and mult_out is read only by Accumulator.

Module Accumulator — the multiply fused with the per-row accumulation onto the block

  • Deleted reg [15:0] wr_data; and reg [15:0] wr_data_delay; (old lines 3358, 3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (rst branch), wr_data_delay <= wr_data;, wr_data <= mult_out; (row-load branch), wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (accumulate branch). wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, both *_rd_en_reg registers and the whole branch structure are untouched.
  • Added one mac_int_18x19 u_mac per Accumulator (32 instances, one per channel) with every input port connected by name: .clk(clk), .reset(rst) (the single design reset net, so all 32 blocks share it and two pack per DSP tile → 16 of 132 tiles), .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}), .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}), .out(mac_out). The block's operand register replaces wr_data's stage and its running sum replaces wr_data_delay's stage, so the number of stages from vec_val/mat_val to fifo_data_out.din is unchanged.
  • a is ungated (mult_out[15:8], the vec_val half, forced to 0 only on an x word); b is the mat_val half gated by mac_en, so a non-accumulate cycle adds the term a_reg*0 = 0 — the block has no enable and reproduces "hold" with a zero term.
  • Added acc_base/load_d: load_d is the row-load condition delayed one cycle, and acc_base captures mac_out[15:0] at the load, so mac_out[15:0] - acc_base reproduces the original wr_data_delay mod 2^16 (products ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, block wraps at 2^37, so the truncate-and-subtract is exact). Both are cleared by rst, before wr_en can be 1.
  • Added x_mult/x_data/x_data_delay to carry the original's all-x wr_data behaviour outside the block (an x can never leave the block's enable-less sum, but the original drops it at the next row load). x_data <= 1'b0; sits where wr_data<=0; was, x_data_delay <= x_data; where wr_data_delay <= wr_data; was, x_data <= x_mult; and x_data <= x_data | x_mult; in the two accumulate branches. Under SYNTHESIS x_mult is constant 0 and this folds away.
  • fifo_data_out's .din is now wr_data_emul. fifo_addr_out, both FIFOs' control, and everything outside Channel/Accumulator are unchanged; no module ports were added.

No ASSUMES: line: the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under mid-run resets — rst clears the block's operand registers, running sum, acc_base and load_d, and wr_en is held 0 from the reset edge until start re-asserts and the ~first_read branch has run, which is exactly the window in which the emulated value is not yet meaningful.

Cell census after the edit: mac_int_18x19 32, no $mul cells remaining, $sub 32 (the per-channel - acc_base).

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH = 8;
	// parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = `LOG2(FIFO_DEPTH);
	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(8);

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult;
	reg mult_wr_en;
	wire mult_full;
	
	reg vec_val_rd_en_reg;
	reg mat_val_rd_en_reg;

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.dw(`MULT_BITS),
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS)

		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),

module Accumulator(
	input clk, 
	input rst, 
	input start, 
	input mult_done,

	input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id,
	input row_id_empty,
	output row_id_rd_en,

	input [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	input mult_empty,
	output mult_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out,
	output data_out_empty,
	output [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out,
	input out_rd_en,

	output reg done
	);
	
	reg first_read;

	reg row_id_rd_en_reg;
	reg mult_rd_en_reg;

	wire data_out_full;
	wire addr_out_full_nc;

	wire addr_out_empty_nc;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr;
	reg wr_en;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr_delay;

	reg last;

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(`FIFO_DEPTH);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`MULT_BITS)
	) fifo_data_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_data_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(data_out),
		.full(data_out_full),
		.empty(data_out_empty)
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`ROW_ID_BITS)
	) fifo_addr_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_addr_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(addr_out),
		.full(addr_out_full_nc),
		.empty(addr_out_empty_nc)
	);

	assign row_id_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);
	assign mult_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			wr_data<=0;
			wr_en<=0;
			first_read <= 0;
			done <= 0;
		end 
		else if(start) begin
			last <= mult_done & row_id_empty;
			row_id_rd_en_reg <= row_id_rd_en;
			mult_rd_en_reg <= mult_rd_en;
			wr_addr_delay <= wr_addr;
			wr_data_delay <= wr_data;

			if(~first_read) begin
				wr_addr <= row_id;
				first_read <= 1;
			end 		
			else if(row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg) begin
				if(row_id!=wr_addr) begin
					wr_en <= 1;
					wr_data <= mult_out;
					wr_addr <= row_id;
				end
				else begin
					wr_en <= 0;
					wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out;
				end
			end
			else if (last) begin
				wr_en <= 1;
				done <= 1;
			end
			else begin
				wr_en <= 0;
			end
		end
	end
endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.

The design

The file spmv.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source and the kept candidate's context before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per Accumulator instance (32 total, one per channel): .clk(clk) ← Accumulator.clk; .reset(rst) ← Accumulator.rst (the single design reset net, shared by all 32 blocks, so two blocks pack per DSP tile → 16 tiles); .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}) ← the vec_val half of the fifo_mult word, ungated (mult_out[15:8]); .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}) ← the mat_val half, gated by the accumulate condition; .out(mac_out[36:0]) → mac_out[15:0] - acc_base becomes the din of fifo_data_out, replacing wr_data_delay.

PLAN:

This pass keeps the structure of the measured candidate (which stands at A/A0 0.916, D/D0 0.959, product 0.878) and changes one thing: the a operand of each block is no longer gated by the accumulate enable. Everything else — the move of the multiply across fifo_mult, the acc_base/load_d emulation of wr_data_delay, the x bookkeeping, the deletion of wr_data/wr_data_delay — is reproduced exactly as in the kept edit.

iddecisionreason
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances)converted (T2)as below
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13, bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57left — correctnesseach is counter*CONST used only as a shift amount (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) at spmv.v:163, addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) at spmv.v:172, id >> (counter*8) in bvb at spmv.v:2490). The product is consumed combinationally in the same cycle by the shifter; there is no register stage between the multiply and its reader for the block's two mandatory register stages to occupy, so putting it in the block would delay the shifted value by 2 cycles and change behaviour. They are also multiplies by a power of two (free wiring today), so converting them could only add a DSP tile and logic.
The rewrite (full description; the editor applies it to the unmodified spmv.v)
Why the product is moved across fifo_mult

In Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323) the product has exactly one register stage (mult <= vec_val * mat_val, operands already registered FIFO outputs). The block needs two, and there is no second soft register there to absorb, so an in-place rewrite would write fifo_mult a cycle late. The consumer side does have two stages: mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay → fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3423, 3428, 3414, 3376). So the operand pair travels through fifo_mult (which is MULT_BITS = 16 bits wide, exactly the two 8-bit operands — no width, pointer, full/empty or timing change), and the multiply is fused with the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out inside the block: the block's operand register takes the place of wr_data, its accumulator register the place of wr_data_delay. mult_out is read by nothing but Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428 via the Channel_Accumulator wiring), so changing what the FIFO word carries is invisible elsewhere; an unwritten slot is 0 in both designs ($init_zero), product 0.

Edit 1 — Channel, spmv.v:3317
verilog
mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};      // was: mult <= vec_val * mat_val;

Nothing else in Channel changes (mult_wr_en, the vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg enable, the FIFO are untouched). mult is only fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and the FIFO writes exactly on the cycles where mult was just loaded, so the held value is never observed, as before.

Edit 2 — Accumulator, spmv.v:3326-3440

Delete the declarations of wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and the statements wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423), wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, both rd_en registers and the whole branch structure exactly as they are (the load branch becomes begin wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; end). Change fifo_data_out's data port (spmv.v:3376) to .din(wr_data_emul). Add:

verilog
	wire [36:0] mac_out;
	reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
	reg  load_d;

	// x bookkeeping, as in the measured candidate: the original's product is all-x when
	// either operand has an x bit; here the FIFO word carries the operand pair, so that
	// case is "this word has an x bit". The block's running sum has no load and no enable,
	// so an x that entered it could never leave, while the original's wr_data drops its x
	// at the next row load; the x is therefore kept out of the block and carried by
	// x_data/x_data_delay. Under synthesis x_mult is constant 0 and all of this folds away.
`ifdef SYNTHESIS
	wire x_mult = 1'b0;
`else
	wire x_mult = ((^mult_out) === 1'bx);
`endif
	reg x_data;        // 1 when the original's wr_data would be all-x
	reg x_data_delay;  // 1 when the original's wr_data_delay would be all-x

	// exactly the condition under which the original updated wr_data
	wire acc_en   = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
	wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
	wire mac_en   = acc_en & (~x_mult);

	// CHANGE FROM THE MEASURED CANDIDATE: `a` is not gated by mac_en. Zeroing `b` alone
	// already makes the block's term 0 on a non-accumulate cycle, so the 8 gating LUTs on
	// the `a` side (8 per channel, 256 over the design) are dropped. The x_mult term is
	// the only guard left on `a`, and it is constant 0 in synthesis, so `a` becomes plain
	// wires from the FIFO word to the block.
	wire [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_a_in = x_mult ? {`VEC_VAL_BITS{1'b0}} : mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS];
	wire [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_b_in = mac_en ? mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] : {`MAT_VAL_BITS{1'b0}};

	mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
		.clk(clk),
		.reset(rst),
		.a({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
		.b({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
		.out(mac_out)
	);

	// out - acc_base reproduces the original wr_data_delay (mod 2^MULT_BITS)
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = x_data_delay ? {`MULT_BITS{1'bx}}
	                                                  : (mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			acc_base <= 0;
			load_d   <= 0;
		end
		else begin
			load_d <= load_now;
			if(load_d) acc_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
		end
	end

and in the existing always block put x_data <= 1'b0; where wr_data<=0; was (rst branch), x_data_delay <= x_data; where wr_data_delay <= wr_data; was, x_data <= x_mult; where wr_data <= mult_out; was, and x_data <= x_data | x_mult; where wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; was.

No new module ports, no change outside Channel and Accumulator.

Why the ungated a is still cycle-for-cycle identical

The block's term is a_reg*b_reg. On any cycle with mac_en = 0 the editor drives b = 0, so b_reg = 0 at the next edge and the term added is a_reg*0 = 0 whatever a_reg holds — the same zero term the gated version added. a_reg is never read except through that product. The only case where a stale a_reg could matter in simulation is an x operand, and a is still forced to 0 whenever x_mult is 1, so a_reg is never x and a_reg*0 never evaluates to x. On a cycle with mac_en = 1 both operands are the FIFO word's halves, exactly as before. The block's two register stages, the accumulate timing, and every other signal are unchanged, so the equivalence argument of the measured candidate carries over verbatim:

  • out(j+1) = out(j) + mult_out(j-1)·[acc_en(j-1)], against the original's wr_data(j) = wr_data(j-1) + prod(mult_out(j-1)) under the identical condition; the block has no enable and reproduces "hold" by adding a zero term.
  • Invariant out(j) - acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j-1) = wr_data_delay(j) (mod 2^16). acc_base is unchanged on accumulate/hold; on a load the original does wr_data <= mult_out, so the new base must be out(j), which load_d (the load condition delayed one cycle) captures at the next edge.
  • Modulus: products are ≤ 255·255 < 2^16, the block wraps at 2^37, wr_data at 2^16; taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting acc_base mod 2^16 is exact.
  • Reset (reset ← rst, synchronous active high, same net for all 32 blocks): a high rst at cycles r1..r2 clears out, a_reg, b_reg, acc_base and load_d at edges r1+1..r2+1, matching the original's wr_data<=0. The invariant is momentarily off by one cycle (the original's wr_data_delay keeps its pre-reset value at r1+1, since the rst branch does not touch it), but the rst branch also forces wr_en<=0, and wr_en can only be re-asserted inside else if(start); start (spmv.v:143-154) is cleared by rst and only re-asserts after all_empty drops, and at the first start cycle s the ~first_read branch runs and does not touch wr_en. So wr_en(j)=0 for every j in [r1+1, s+1] — exactly the window where the emulated value is not yet meaningful — and the invariant is re-established at s+1 (out=0, acc_base=0, wr_data(s)=0). The last branch raises wr_en without changing wr_data, and there acc_en=0, so the block holds too.
  • Power-up: the block's sum is X until the first clear; the testbench holds rst for its first 4 cycles (before comparison starts) and wr_en is 0 throughout, so no X reaches fifo_data_out. acc_base and load_d are added registers whose initial value is not relied on — both are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equivalent, including under the testbench's mid-run 1-, 2- and 3-cycle resets.

Blocks and register stages
  • Blocks added: 32, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and the one rst net, so two blocks fit per DSP tile: 16 tiles of 132 — unchanged from the measured candidate, so the expected delta is soft logic only (256 LUT2s of operand gating removed, ≈26 CLBs).

  • Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75, operands vec_val/mat_val to the value entering fifo_data_out:

    • before: 1 soft stage (mult) → fifo_mult → 2 soft stages (wr_data, wr_data_delay).
    • after: 1 soft stage (mult, now carrying the operand pair, multiply removed) → fifo_mult → 2 block stages (operand register, accumulator register), plus the combinational 16-bit - acc_base on the FIFO's din.
    • Total stages, and the cycle on which each FIFO is written, are unchanged; the multiply moved from before fifo_mult to after it.

Note on where the remaining delay is: at seed 1 the reported worst path is entirely soft (an enabled flop through five .names levels into a top-level flop, dominated by wire delay — the global |fetcher_empty / &done style reductions in spmv). It does not touch the block, so no block-side change can shorten it; only the density gained from fewer tiles and less soft logic can.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if dpram exposed a combinational (pre-register) read of port A, so generic_fifo_sc_a could offer "the word dout will carry next cycle", the vec_val*mat_val product could be mapped inside Channel itself: the FIFO's output register would become the block's operand register and the block's accumulator register would become mult, with the product recovered as the difference of two consecutive sums. That needs the pre-register output plumbed from the 32 bvb and 32 fetcher FIFOs through spmv/Big_Channel/Channel_Accumulator, and is a change to the design, not to this rewrite.

Simulation

simulation passed: 17311 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows that every output matches on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles); it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the design source and supporting files.

ACCEPT

Plan conformance — the edit touches exactly the two regions the plan names, and nothing else:

  • spmv.v:3317 — mult <= {vec_val, mat_val}; replaces the product; mult is 16 bits (MULT_BITS = 8+8) and {vec_val,mat_val} is 16 bits, so fifo_mult width, pointers, full/empty and write timing are unchanged.
  • Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3486) — wr_data/wr_data_delay and their four statements removed, x_data/x_data_delay put in their exact slots, block + acc_base/load_d added, fifo_data_out.din → wr_data_emul. wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, wr_en, first_read, last, done, both rd_en registers and the branch structure are untouched, as the plan says. Remaining diff hunks are whitespace only. Grep confirms no dangling wr_data/wr_data_delay reference in Accumulator (the top-level wr_data at :133/:175/:212 is a different module).

Ports — all four block inputs connected by name (spmv.v:3393-3399): .clk(clk), .reset(rst) (the single design reset net, so all 32 blocks share clk/reset → 2 per tile, 16 of 132 tiles), .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}) = 18 b, .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}) = 19 b. .out(mac_out) drives only wr_data_emul → fifo_data_out.din, as planned.

Visibility — mult_out is read only by Accumulator (spmv.v:3222/3235/3248 wire it Channel→Accumulator inside Channel_Accumulator; no other reader), so re-purposing the FIFO word to the operand pair is invisible outside. wr_data/wr_data_delay had no reader but fifo_data_out.din.

Terms / arithmetic — one product per accumulate cycle, zero-extended unsigned operands; 255*255 < 2^16, and 2^37 is a multiple of 2^16, so mac_out[15:0] - acc_base reproduces the original mod-2^16 wr_data_delay exactly.

Registers and reset — the two block stages take the places of wr_data and wr_data_delay. Checked the invariant out(j) − acc_base(j) ≡ wr_data(j−1) = wr_data_delay(j): hold cycles add a_reg·0 = 0 (mac_b_in forced 0 when acc_en=0); accumulate cycles add prod(j−1) with acc_base unchanged; load cycles re-anchor acc_base(j+1) = out(j) via load_d, giving prod(j−1) = wr_data(j). row_id != wr_addr evaluating to x makes load_d x, and if(load_d) then holds acc_base — the same "else" (accumulate) choice the original's if makes, so the x case is consistent too. start (spmv.v:143-154) only ever rises outside reset, so the "start falls with wr_en stuck high" hazard does not exist. After any rst the invariant is briefly off (the original holds wr_data_delay), but rst forces wr_en<=0, start<=0, first_read<=0, and the first start cycle takes the ~first_read branch, so wr_en=0 across the whole window and the invariant is re-established with out=acc_base=wr_data=0.

Ungated a — sound: a_reg is read only through a_reg·b_reg, and b_reg is 0 on every non-accumulate cycle; mac_a_in is still forced to 0 on x_mult, so a_reg is never x and a_reg·0 never evaluates to x.

X handling — (^mult_out) === 1'bx is true exactly when some operand bit is x, which is exactly when the original's * and + produced all-x; x_data/x_data_delay carry that, and the forced-x wr_data_emul matches. The skipped x term never corrupts the sum because the next row load re-anchors acc_base. `ifdef SYNTHESIS folds it to constant 0 for the 2-state path. Added registers' initial values are not relied on (acc_base, load_d, x_data are cleared by rst before wr_en can be 1; x_data_delay is only consumed under wr_en), consistent with x on dut only: 0 in sim_result.json.

Scope / assumptions — no new module ports, no change outside Channel and Accumulator, and no ASSUMES: line is needed since the equivalence is unconditional.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

3530	//  CVS Log
3531	//
3532	//  $Id: generic_fifo_sc_a.v,v 1.1.1.1 2002-09-25 05:42:06 rudi Exp $
3533	//
3534	//  $Date: 2002-09-25 05:42:06 $
3535	//  $Revision: 1.1.1.1 $

3537	//  $Locker:  $
3538	//  $State: Exp $
3539	//
3540	// Change History:
3541	//               $Log: not supported by cvs2svn $
