3547	//
3548	//
3549	//
3550	//
3551	//
3552	
3553	// `include "timescale.v"
3554	
3555	/*
3556	
3557	Description
3558	===========
3559	
3560	I/Os
3561	----
3562	rst	low active, either sync. or async. master reset (see below how to select)
3563	clr	synchronous clear (just like reset but always synchronous), high active
3564	re	read enable, synchronous, high active
3565	we	read enable, synchronous, high active
3566	din	Data Input
3567	dout	Data Output
3568	
3569	full	Indicates the FIFO is full (combinatorial output)
3570	full_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3571	empty	Indicates the FIFO is empty
3572	empty_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3573	
3574	full_n		Indicates if the FIFO has space for N entries (combinatorial output)
3575	full_n_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3576	empty_n		Indicates the FIFO has at least N entries (combinatorial output)
3577	empty_n_r	same as above, but registered output (see note below)
3578	
3579	level		indicates the FIFO level:
3580			2'b00	0-25%	 full
3581			2'b01	25-50%	 full
3582			2'b10	50-75%	 full
3583			2'b11	%75-100% full
3584	
3585	combinatorial vs. registered status outputs
3586	-------------------------------------------
3587	Both the combinatorial and registered status outputs have exactly the same
3588	synchronous timing. Meaning they are being asserted immediately at the clock
3589	edge after the last read or write. The combinatorial outputs however, pass
3590	through several levels of logic before they are output. The registered status
3591	outputs are direct outputs of a flip-flop. The reason both are provided, is
3592	that the registered outputs require quite a bit of additional logic inside
3593	the FIFO. If you can meet timing of your device with the combinatorial
3594	outputs, use them ! The FIFO will be smaller. If the status signals are
3595	in the critical pass, use the registered outputs, they have a much smaller
3596	output delay (actually only Tcq).
3597	
3598	Parameters
3599	----------
3600	The FIFO takes 3 parameters:
3601	dw	Data bus width
3602	aw	Address bus width (Determines the FIFO size by evaluating 2^aw)
3603	n	N is a second status threshold constant for full_n and empty_n
3604		If you have no need for the second status threshold, do not
3605		connect the outputs and the logic should be removed by your
3606		synthesis tool.
3607	
3608	Synthesis Results
3609	-----------------
3610	In a Spartan 2e a 8 bit wide, 8 entries deep FIFO, takes 85 LUTs and runs
3611	at about 116 MHz (IO insertion disabled). The registered status outputs
3612	are valid after 2.1NS, the combinatorial once take out to 6.5 NS to be
3613	available.
3614	
3615	
3616	Misc
3617	----
3618	This design assumes you will do appropriate status checking externally.
3619	
3620	IMPORTANT ! writing while the FIFO is full or reading while the FIFO is
3621	empty will place the FIFO in an undefined state.
362
…[truncated, 4743 chars total]

parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(`FIFO_DEPTH);
+ // ---- hard MAC: the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + vec_val*mat_val ----
+ // mult_out carries the operand pair {vec_val, mat_val} written by Channel.
+ wire [36:0] mac_out;
+ reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
+ reg load_d;
+
+ // x-ness of the original's product. The original multiplies in Channel, so its fifo
+ // word is all-x whenever either operand has an x bit; here the word carries the
+ // operand pair, so that case is exactly "this word has an x bit". The block's running
+ // sum has no load and no enable, so an x that reached it could never leave it, while
+ // the original's wr_data drops its x at the next row load. The x is therefore kept out
+ // of the block and carried by x_data/x_data_delay below instead. Hardware has no x:
+ // under synthesis x_mult is constant 0, x_data/x_data_delay fold to 0 and all of this
+ // disappears, leaving exactly the plain gating.
+`ifdef SYNTHESIS
+ wire x_mult = 1'b0;
+`else
+ wire x_mult = ((^mult_out) === 1'bx);
+`endif
+ reg x_data; // 1 when the original's wr_data would be all-x
+ reg x_data_delay; // 1 when the original's wr_data_delay would be all-x
+
+ // exactly the condition under which the original updated wr_data
+ wire acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
+ wire load_now = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
+ wire mac_en = acc_en & (~x_mult);
+
+ wire [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_a_in = mac_en ? mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS] : {`VEC_VAL_BITS{1'b0}};
+ wire [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mac_b_in = mac_en ? mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] : {`MAT_VAL_BITS{1'b0}};
+
+ mac_int_18x19 u_mac (
+ .clk(clk),
+ .reset(rst),
+ .a({10'b0, mac_a_in}),
+ .b({11'b0, mac_b_in}),
+ .out(mac_out)
+ );
+
+ // out - acc_base reproduces the original wr_data_delay (mod 2^MULT_BITS)
+ wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_emul = x_data_delay ? {`MULT_BITS{1'bx}}

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -4.57% against first, min-gain 0.089 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 5.85603e+07 · critical path 6.95433 ns · wirelength 197938 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 4.07248e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 8.90%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 8.90%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

+ // ---- hard MAC: the per-row accumulation wr_data <= wr_data + vec_val*mat_val ----
+ // mult_out carries the operand pair {vec_val, mat_val} written by Channel.
+ wire [36:0] mac_out;
+ reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
+ reg load_d;
+
+ // x-ness of the original's product. The original multiplies in Channel, so its fifo
+ // word is all-x whenever either operand has an x bit; here the word carries the
+ // operand pair, so that case is exactly "this word has an x bit". The block's running
+ // sum has no load and no enable, so an x that reached it could never leave it, while
+ // the original's wr_data drops its x at the next row load. The x is therefore kept out
+ // of the block and carried by x_data/x_data_delay below instead. Hardware has no x:
+ // under synthesis x_mult is constant 0, x_data/x_data_delay fold to 0 and all of this
+ // disappears, leaving exactly the plain gating.
+`ifdef SYNTHESIS
+ wire x_mult = 1'b0;
+`else
+ wire x_mult = ((^mult_out) === 1'bx);
+`endif
+ reg x_data; // 1 when the original's wr_data would be all-x
+ reg x_data_delay; // 1 when the original's wr_data_delay would be all-x
+
+ // exactly the condition under which the original updated wr_data

+ wire mac_en = acc_en & (~x_mult);
+
